    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/new-requirement/outputs/evidence/source/input__README.md",
      "bytes": 237,
      "sha256": "0f788464b62966cab555b5cf2ef73ad70af5cae94e71b24aaccae4719c0f6a86",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-new-requirement",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Single tenant inventory\nOne in-memory Inventory stores SKU quantities for a single company. Item identity is currently only SKU; callers share one store. API: put/get/remove. Types and example are part of the affected public boundary.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/new-requirement/outputs/evidence/source/skills__principle-redesign-from-first-principles__SKILL.md",
      "bytes": 1556,
      "sha256": "e97f14b193dc65815e101e192ffa0ffec3507f27df212d067bfb7d1e6bc44818",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-new-requirement",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: principle-redesign-from-first-principles\ndescription: \"Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Redesign as if the requirement had been a foundational assumption from day one, instead of bolting it on.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Redesign From First Principles\n\nWhen integrating a change, don't bolt it onto the existing design. Redesign as if the requirement had been there from the start.\n\n- Read all affected files and understand the current design\n- Ask: \"if we were writing this from scratch with this new requirement, what would we build?\"\n- Propagate the change through every reference: types, docs, examples, rationale sections\n- Think about the whole redesign, then deliver it incrementally\n\nThis is the method for preserving option value when integrating changes into an existing design.\n\n## Capability and permission boundaries\n\nUse the [session capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) before reading source, editing files or running checks. Apply the redesign within the authorized task. Propagating references does not grant permission to change unrelated files or perform external actions.\n\nIf source is available but writes or execution are unavailable, provide a concrete redesign and incremental sequence as reduced capability. State which edits and checks were not performed. If affected source or design context is missing, identify the missing inputs and keep any redesign provisional. Claim implementation or verification only after observing the authorized operation.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/new-requirement/outputs/evidence/source/skills__architect__SKILL.md",
      "bytes": 7555,
      "sha256": "af93988bb4eec01a50f1caf71fd196a01f5affd56d2dcb36fe70d1735fa28f78",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-new-requirement",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: architect\ndescription: \"Sketch types, signatures, and module structure before code, then stay in the loop while implementation fills in. Use for /architect, 'architect this', 'design this', or non-trivial work where jumping to code would lock in the wrong shape.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Architect\n\nDesign before implementing. Sketch types, function signatures, class shapes, and module boundaries with `not implemented` bodies and pseudocode. Synthesize across multiple model perspectives, then fill in code against the chosen sketch. If implementation proves the sketch wrong, throw it out and redesign.\n\n## Start\n\nOpen a todolist with one entry per phase before starting. If no task-list tool is exposed, keep the same phase list in the response and disclose that limit.\n\n1. Ground\n2. Sketch\n3. Agree\n4. Implement\n5. Scrap\n\n## Session capabilities and authorization\n\nRead [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md) and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Use only observed exposed tools within user authorization. Read and follow each required skill in full. If direct invocation is unavailable, disclose that limit and execute its complete workflow inline as reduced capability. A missing required skill remains blocked. Record worker identities, requested and observed selections, rejections, result artifacts and limits.\n\nIndependent exploration requires authorized workers with observed spawn and result inspection. Without workers, disclose reduced capability and produce at least two sequential, structurally distinct alternatives using the complete arena workflow and runner discipline. Sequential alternatives are not independent model perspectives. Unverified or identical observed models do not establish model diversity. Denied source reads block grounding; denied writes leave a read-only design and an explicit implementation block.\n\n## Phase A: Ground the problem\n\nBuild a real mental model of every system the new code touches. Run the **how** skill over the relevant subsystems.\n\nNaming a file isn't grounding. Produce the traced model `how` prescribes. If the design redefines ownership or layering, also run the **why** skill on the existing shape so the rationale becomes a constraint, not a guess.\n\nSkip Phase A only when the work is genuinely greenfield with no surrounding system to integrate.\n\n## Phase B: Sketch\n\nRun the **arena** skill with the design-sketch task and the Phase A grounding artifacts. Pass `references/runner-prompt.md` as each runner's prompt. Each candidate produces a design package shaped per `references/rationale-template.md`.\n\nTake runner preferences from an available user-supplied `architect runners` configuration, in place of arena runner preferences. When no architect runner preferences are supplied, request inherited selection per the model-role contract. Preserve the goal of distinct model perspectives when supported and verified; inheritance does not establish diversity. Resolve aliases and rejected entries through the complete **arena** Phase A rules and current model-role contract. Select explicit models only when the exposed operation supports them; otherwise disclose inherited selection and unverified diversity. If an exact selection is required but unavailable, block that selection instead of silently replacing it.\n\nDesign it twice. Require at least two structurally distinct candidates before synthesis, even when the first looks sufficient. This is the **exhaust-the-design-space** principle skill made concrete. Whole-shape alternatives, not point fixes inside one shape.\n\nScreen every candidate against [`references/design-red-flags.md`](references/design-red-flags.md) before synthesis. Assume the next contributor is an agent that sees only the files it opened, copies the nearest example, and takes the shortest path that compiles. Prefer the design where a change that looks right from one file is right for the whole repo.\n\nCompare viable candidates on interface depth. Prefer the design that hides more complexity behind a smaller, simpler public surface. A rich interface can keep call chains short by concentrating capability instead of scattering it across layers.\n\nArena returns one synthesized design package. The synthesis decision populates the rationale's \"Synthesis decision\" section.\n\n## Phase C: Agree (opt-in)\n\nDefault: proceed directly to implementation with the synthesized design when implementation is already authorized. No additional human checkpoint. Design-only scope does not authorize implementation.\n\nOpt in to a checkpoint when the invoker explicitly asks: \"/architect with checkpoint,\" \"stop and show me before implementing,\" or similar. Then surface the synthesized design and pause for sign-off.\n\nWith explicit commit authorization, the synthesis can ship as its own commit either way, as the \"scaffold first\" mode of the **foundational-thinking** principle skill. Planned and scoped breakage during fill-in is fine, per the **outcome-oriented-execution** principle skill. For adversarial pressure on the design before implementing, run the **interrogate** skill on the synthesized sketch.\n\nIf the human pushes back on the shape (in a checkpoint or after the fact), treat that as Phase A evidence. Re-ground and re-run Phase B before writing more code.\n\n## Phase D: Implement against the sketch\n\nReplace `not implemented` bodies with code, pseudocode with logic. The synthesized sketch is the contract.\n\nDeviations from the sketch are signal worth surfacing, not friction to absorb silently. If a function needs a parameter the sketch didn't anticipate, ask whether the sketch was wrong, the requirement was missed, or the implementation is overreaching.\n\n## Phase E: Scrap when the architecture is wrong\n\nIf implementation keeps producing friction the sketch can't absorb, throw the sketch out. Don't bolt fixes onto a wrong design, per the **redesign-from-first-principles** and **fix-root-causes** principle skills.\n\nThe signal is a *pattern*, not single instances. Tells:\n\n- The same shape of workaround appearing repeatedly across unrelated code.\n- Multiple unrelated edge cases that all need special-case branches.\n- Types that need escape hatches (`any`, casts, optional fields always set in practice) to compile.\n- The \"we need a lock\" reflex when the sketch said the state wasn't shared.\n- Callers having to know the abstraction's internal rules to use it.\n- Two or more independent Phase D deviations of the same shape across the implementation.\n\nUse judgment. A few edge cases don't condemn an architecture. Some problems are legitimately complex. Complexity in the data is not complexity in the design.\n\nWhen you scrap:\n\n1. Re-run the **how** skill over what's been built.\n2. Redesign as if the new constraints had been day-one assumptions, per redesign-from-first-principles.\n3. Subtract before adding, per the **subtract-before-you-add** principle skill. The new sketch should be smaller than the old one before it grows.\n4. Return to Phase B and re-run arena.\n\n## Outputs\n\nThe caller's usage is written first and the type sketch derived from it. One file with new types and signatures for small changes. Module map plus type definitions for larger work. The rationale ships alongside, shaped per `references/rationale-template.md`, including the usage sketch and the synthesis decision.\n\nApply [unslop](../unslop/SKILL.md) to generated rationale and reports without changing facts or principle meanings.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/new-requirement/outputs/evidence/source/skills__typescript-best-practices__SKILL.md",
      "bytes": 3959,
      "sha256": "c9495954ee2ce4462d10a8b1aa2d82e1b80167449fe9dc0ccee6b41c184f8983",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-new-requirement",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: typescript-best-practices\ndescription: TypeScript best practices. Use when reading or editing any .ts or .tsx file.\npaths: [\"**/*.ts\", \"**/*.tsx\"]\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# TypeScript best practices\n\nApply the **type-system-discipline** principle skill first.\n\n| Rule | Summary |\n|------|---------|\n| Discriminated unions | Model variants with a `kind` literal discriminant so impossible states can't be represented. No optional-field bags. |\n| Branded types | Brand primitives with `& { readonly __brand: \"X\" }` so they can't be mixed up. Validate once at the boundary. |\n| Constructive modeling | Build the shape so the illegal value can't be constructed. `[T, ...T[]]` for non-empty, `[T, T][]` for even length, `start` plus `duration` for a range. Not a runtime guard, not a wish for refinement types. |\n| Simplest total type | Keep `T[]` while every operation on it stays total. Strengthen to `NonEmpty<T>` only where the loose type forces `!`, a cast, or a \"should never happen\" throw. |\n| `unknown` over `any` | External data is `unknown`. |\n| Schemas before guards | Before hand-writing a property-by-property type guard, use the repository's runtime schema library and infer the type from the schema, such as `z.infer`. |\n| No `as` casts | Every `as` is a runtime crash waiting. Cast only after validation. |\n| Narrowing hierarchy | Discriminant switch > `in` operator > `typeof`/`instanceof` > user-defined type guard > `as`. |\n| Type guards | Must verify the claim. A lying guard is worse than `as` because the bug hides behind a name that says it's safe. Name them `isX` or `hasX`. |\n| Exhaustiveness | Inline `const _exhaustive: never = x;` in default arms so the compiler errors when a new variant is added. |\n| `satisfies` over `as` | Validates the value without widening literal types. |\n| Boundary validation | Parse where data crosses in, into a named domain type. `Record<string, unknown>` (however spelled) stops at that parse. Trust types inside. See the **boundary-discipline** principle skill. |\n| Schema-derived types | Reach for `Pick`/`Omit`/`Parameters`/`ReturnType`/`Awaited`/`typeof` before declaring a new interface. |\n| Object args | Pass objects, not positional, so argument order is self-documenting. Skip on hot paths (per-frame render, tokenizers, parsers). |\n| Real tests | Don't mock what you can run. Prefer the framework's real test primitives with leak/disposable checks, and verify UI in a running build. Mock only what you can't run locally. |\n| Structured telemetry | Prefer structured logger diagnostics with enough context to debug from an id. No `console.log` in shipped code. |\n\nExamples: `references/patterns.md`.\n\n## Session capabilities and permissions\n\nRead [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) for the current session. Read the [type-system-discipline](../principle-type-system-discipline/SKILL.md) principle first, and the [boundary-discipline](../principle-boundary-discipline/SKILL.md) principle for boundary validation. Use the complete [patterns reference](references/patterns.md). If a dependency cannot be read, name it and label the review partial.\n\nApply these practices to supplied code or readable repository files. Saved edits require available write tools and authorization for the affected paths. If writes are denied or unavailable, return a proposed refactor in the conversation and state that it is unsaved. If no TypeScript input can be read or supplied, report the missing input before proceeding.\n\nRun the repository's checks only with available execution tools and authorized commands. Reuse its installed runtime schema library; do not install a dependency for one guard. Report compiler, test, and running UI checks only after observing their results. If execution is denied or unavailable, identify the checks that remain unperformed. Retain the source metadata without assuming that every host enforces paths or disable-model-invocation.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/new-requirement/outputs/evidence/source/references__workflow-ownership.md",
      "bytes": 1265,
      "sha256": "50644b3a9a3dfd5651382eaca1d3a2728af0b3157d6d66b730930370a64bb03c",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-new-requirement",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Workflow ownership\n\nAn explicit pstack invocation gives pstack ownership of that workflow. An explicit Superpowers invocation gives Superpowers ownership. Do not run both controllers over the same assignment or let one silently replace the other's plan, review, or integration decisions.\n\nFor an unqualified request, D1 retains the already active workflow. With no active workflow, use host defaults and do not activate either orchestration mode automatically. If both pstack and Superpowers are explicitly requested, clarify ownership before launching workers or changing artifacts. Preserve the active controller across follow-ups unless the user changes ownership. A helper skill can advise the owner without becoming a second controller.\n\nThe owner assigns one coordinator. Workers produce isolated artifacts and evidence; the coordinator reviews and integrates them. Shared inventory and registry writes belong to the coordinator. Worker cancellation, stale results or unavailable tools do not transfer that ownership automatically.\n\nHost permissions and user authorization constrain every owner. Local structural tests, documented APIs and declared cases provide no host pass. Explicit reduced or blocked results remain visible beside the full expectation.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/new-requirement/outputs/evidence/source/skills__principle-type-system-discipline__SKILL.md",
      "bytes": 5641,
      "sha256": "65649b9c31d43b481ab4cf299ce51441b5077d2cfb964369b151df0889b484e9",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-new-requirement",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: principle-type-system-discipline\ndescription: \"Apply when designing types, reviewing a function signature, or writing code in any statically-typed language. Make illegal states unrepresentable, brand semantic primitives, parse external data at boundaries, refuse to lie to the compiler, exhaust variants, derive from authoritative schemas.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Type System Discipline\n\nThe type checker is a proof assistant. Use it to eliminate impossible states, mismatched primitives, and unhandled variants at compile time. A case the types let you ignore becomes a runtime failure the compiler could have stopped. Prefer defining errors and special cases out of existence over proliferating handlers. Unrepresentable states, total functions, and interface redesign (the patterns below) are the tools.\n\nApplies to any typed language. Skills like `typescript-best-practices` ground it in specific syntax.\n\n**The patterns:**\n\n- **Make illegal states unrepresentable.** Model variants as sum types: discriminated unions in TypeScript, enums with payloads in Rust/Swift/Kotlin, sealed classes in Scala, ADTs in Haskell/OCaml. Don't model state as a bag of optional fields where contradictory combinations compile. A subtle anti-pattern: `{ completed: boolean; completedAt?: Date }` admits `completed: true; completedAt: undefined`, which is meaningless. Derive the boolean from a single source like `completedAt !== null`, or model the variants explicitly as `{ kind: 'open' } | { kind: 'done'; at: Date }`. If a bug forces the question \"wait, can this combination actually happen?\", the type is too loose.\n- **Types are constructions, not restrictions.** Build the type up from the values you want instead of carving them out of a looser type with checks. The invariant that seems to need a refinement type is usually a construction away. A non-empty list is a head plus a rest, not a list with a length check. A valid time range is a start plus a duration, not two timestamps you must keep ordered. No representation is privileged. A list of pairs is an even-length list if you interpret it that way, so choose the shape that cannot build the illegal value and expose the interface callers need on top.\n- **Brand semantic primitives.** `UserId` and `OrderId` are strings underneath but should not be interchangeable. Newtypes in Rust, opaque types in Swift, value classes in Kotlin, phantom types in Haskell, branded intersections in TypeScript. Validate once at creation, trust the type downstream.\n- **External data is untyped until parsed.** RPC payloads, JSON, IPC messages, CLI args, config files, environment variables, database rows. Have a parse function at every boundary that turns unstructured input into the typed model. See the **boundary-discipline** principle skill for where to put validation.\n- **Don't lie to the type system.** Casts, unsafe coercions, and assertion functions that bypass the compiler are latent runtime crashes. If the compiler can't prove a fact, prove it (validate, narrow, refine the model) or accept that the cast is a hazard.\n- **Exhaustive matching is the compiler's job.** When you match on a sum type, the compiler must fail compilation if a new variant is added without handling. Use the idiom your language provides: `never`-typed binding in TypeScript, unannotated `match` in Rust, `-Wincomplete-patterns` in Haskell, sealed-class match exhaustiveness in Kotlin.\n- **Derive types from authoritative schemas.** When a protocol buffer, OpenAPI spec, GraphQL schema, database migration, or design-system token file defines a shape, derive from it instead of hand-rolling a parallel type. See the **encode-lessons-in-structure** principle skill.\n- **Strengthen a type only where partiality appears.** A runtime assertion, null check, or \"this should never happen\" throw marks the place a type is too weak. Push that check up into the type. Then stop. The type system's job is to track the cases each use site must handle, not to describe the data as precisely as possible. Prefer total functions. `sum` of an empty list is 0, so it takes the plain list. `head` of an empty list has no answer, so it demands the non-empty one.\n\n**The tests:**\n\n- \"Can I write a comment explaining when this combination of fields is valid?\" If yes, the type is too loose. Split it into a sum type.\n- \"Do two of my function arguments share a primitive type but mean different things?\" Brand them.\n- \"Where did this `any`, this `as`, this `assertNotNull` come from?\" Trace it to the boundary and validate there instead.\n- \"If a new variant is added next month, will the compiler tell the next agent where to add a case?\" If no, the match isn't exhaustive.\n- \"Is this type duplicating a shape another file owns?\" Derive instead.\n- \"Am I strengthening this type to keep an operation total, or just to be more precise?\" If nothing would otherwise panic, keep the plain type.\n\n## Session capabilities and limits\n\nFollow [host capability discovery](../../references/host-capabilities.md) for source reads, authorized edits and compiler execution. The metadata above preserves the source invocation policy; do not assume the host enforces it.\n\nApply these patterns within the authorized task and exposed tools. If source access, writes or compiler execution are unavailable, provide a reduced proposal and state the missing checks. Identify the actual language limit when an invariant cannot be expressed in its types, and keep the required runtime validation explicit. Do not claim a compiler check ran without observed execution. Missing tools do not authorize installation or external actions.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/new-requirement/outputs/evidence/source/input__tenant.acceptance.test.mjs",
      "bytes": 649,
      "sha256": "15a5e3d1f1d1799b930cf9aea2ff44c7d906542188d8c35dca5cb22f704635a3",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-new-requirement",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import {test,expect} from 'bun:test';import {TenantInventory} from './inventory.mjs';test('tenant-scoped ownership across shared backend',()=>{const shared=new Map();const a=new TenantInventory('tenant-a',shared),b=new TenantInventory('tenant-b',shared);a.put('same-sku',7);b.put('same-sku',2);expect(a.get('same-sku')).toBe(7);expect(b.get('same-sku')).toBe(2);a.remove('same-sku');expect(a.get('same-sku')).toBe(0);expect(b.get('same-sku')).toBe(2);a.put('tenant-b:sku',3);b.put('sku',8);expect(a.get('tenant-b:sku')).toBe(3);expect(b.get('sku')).toBe(8);expect(()=>new TenantInventory('',shared)).toThrow();expect(()=>a.put('s',-1)).toThrow()});\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/new-requirement/outputs/evidence/source/docs__principles__README.md",
      "bytes": 5166,
      "sha256": "2c7b72c13cb90e1716d7968288fb83930188c697f27dbfe61f8cc2844215589a",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-new-requirement",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Principle catalog\n\nGenerated from 51 reviewed leaf records, including 24 principles. No additional root skill is registered.\n\nHost support depends on capabilities, permissions and observed evidence. Inventory review may remain pending, and a not-run result remains untested.\n\nSource inventory: [docs/parity/inventory.json](../parity/inventory.json). Catalog schema: [docs/catalog/schema.json](../catalog/schema.json).\n\n| Name | Purpose |\n| --- | --- |\n| [principle-attack-the-premise](../skills/principle-attack-the-premise.md) | Question a shared premise after repeated failed fixes, census actor imbalance and remove persistent role asymmetry. |\n| [principle-boundary-discipline](../skills/principle-boundary-discipline.md) | Concentrate validation, type narrowing and error handling at system boundaries; trust internal types and keep business logic in pure functions. |\n| [principle-build-the-lever](../skills/principle-build-the-lever.md) | Build the smallest rerunnable tool that performs or proves nontrivial work, making its result reviewable. |\n| [principle-encode-lessons-in-structure](../skills/principle-encode-lessons-in-structure.md) | Encode recurring corrections in enforceable structures and close the correction loop. |\n| [principle-exhaust-the-design-space](../skills/principle-exhaust-the-design-space.md) | Explore distinct alternatives before choosing a novel interaction or architectural approach. |\n| [principle-experience-first](../skills/principle-experience-first.md) | Choose user delight over implementation convenience, justify scope and polish the central workflow. |\n| [principle-explain-the-number](../skills/principle-explain-the-number.md) | Check that a measured number describes completed work and has an evidenced limiting factor before trusting, reporting or acting on it. |\n| [principle-fix-root-causes](../skills/principle-fix-root-causes.md) | Reproduce debugging symptoms, trace their root causes and fix the causes. |\n| [principle-foundational-thinking](../skills/principle-foundational-thinking.md) | Choose core data, access patterns and ownership before logic; sequence foundations so later work stays simple. |\n| [principle-guard-the-context-window](../skills/principle-guard-the-context-window.md) | Protect the finite context window by isolating large payloads, keeping frequently used content inline and bounding phases. |\n| [principle-laziness-protocol](../skills/principle-laziness-protocol.md) | Prefer deletion and the smallest change that preserves required behavior. |\n| [principle-make-operations-idempotent](../skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent.md) | Make repeated and interrupted operations converge to the correct state through reconciliation. |\n| [principle-migrate-callers-then-delete-legacy-apis](../skills/principle-migrate-callers-then-delete-legacy-apis.md) | Migrate internal callers and delete the old API in the same refactor wave. |\n| [principle-minimize-reader-load](../skills/principle-minimize-reader-load.md) | Reduce the layers readers trace and the hidden or mutable state they must hold to understand code. |\n| [principle-model-the-domain](../skills/principle-model-the-domain.md) | Encode domain rules in concrete structures that remove invalid states, repeated assumptions and scattered branches. |\n| [principle-never-block-on-the-human](../skills/principle-never-block-on-the-human.md) | Proceed on authorized reversible work, present reviewable results and let the human course-correct asynchronously while preserving confirmation and explicit review gates. |\n| [principle-outcome-oriented-execution](../skills/principle-outcome-oriented-execution.md) | Converge on the intended verifiable migration end state without retaining throwaway compatibility states. |\n| [principle-prove-it-works](../skills/principle-prove-it-works.md) | Verify task outputs against the real artifact before declaring completion. |\n| [principle-redesign-from-first-principles](../skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles.md) | Integrate a requirement by redesigning the affected structure as if the requirement had been present from the start, preserving option value. |\n| [principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state](../skills/principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state.md) | Eliminate shared mutable write targets before structurally serializing access to a required shared object. |\n| [principle-sequence-verifiable-units](../skills/principle-sequence-verifiable-units.md) | Sequence work and delivery into small units that each end in a verified state. |\n| [principle-subtract-before-you-add](../skills/principle-subtract-before-you-add.md) | Remove obsolete behavior and redundant complexity before adding its replacement, then build on the simpler base. |\n| [principle-test-behavior-not-implementation](../skills/principle-test-behavior-not-implementation.md) | Keep tests that observe user-visible behavior and catch defects instead of asserting implementation details. |\n| [principle-type-system-discipline](../skills/principle-type-system-discipline.md) | Use types to exclude invalid states, distinguish semantic IDs and require complete handling of variants. |\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/new-requirement/outputs/evidence/source/references__model-roles.md",
      "bytes": 2869,
      "sha256": "c86696b6c568b7db80a7e339856986e64482ce6a418bb91946ce3a3b772f79a1",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-new-requirement",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Model selection for portable roles\n\nUse [session capabilities](host-capabilities.md) before assigning a role. These [poteto](agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and [comment reviewer](agent-prompts/comment-sicko.md) prompts are instructions supplied to an exposed worker operation. Reading them does not register a custom agent or authorize delegation.\n\n| Role | Default requested model | Observed model | Supported effort | Fallback disclosure |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| poteto-agent | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\n| comment-sicko | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\n\nFor each assignment, record requested model and effort, observed model and effort, supported effort values and their source, worker identity, and fallback disclosure. Use `unknown` for information the operation does not expose. `inherit-parent` records a selection policy, not an observed model name. Do not silently convert a requested model into an actual model.\n\nSelect an explicit model only from current exposed tool documentation or observed enumeration results. If enumeration is missing, disclose that limit and retain `inherit-parent`. Documentation examples are not a list of available models. Use only an effort supported for the selected model by the current operation; do not carry an incompatible inherited effort into an explicit selection.\n\nIf a selected model or effort is rejected, retain the rejection. Use an authorized inherited or supported alternative only after disclosing the changed selection. If the task requires that exact selection, stop and record blocked. A fallback never proves the original selection succeeded.\n\nIdentical observed models mean no model diversity. Different roles can supply distinct instructions on the same model. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified, even when requested model names differ. Different observed models support a model-diversity statement only for those observed assignments. Worker independence still requires successful spawn and result inspection.\n\nWhen workers are unavailable, apply an authorized role sequentially and label reduced capability. Preserve scope and permission boundaries. Do not claim independent review, background execution, or full worker support from sequential role switching.\n\nThe cached documentation from https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/subagents describes inheritance and optional local agent configuration. It is context only. This package writes no local custom-agent configuration and claims no current host model or worker support from that documentation.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/new-requirement/outputs/evidence/source/request.md",
      "bytes": 815,
      "sha256": "a8e79daf20b8a02f8e64d90eb3daabc397916a8e6762b0f8cb53749629ba645a",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-new-requirement",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# New authorized tenant requirement\nRedesign the inventory domain as though tenant ownership existed from day one. Two TenantInventory(tenantId, sharedBackendMap) instances must isolate identical SKU names even when sharing storage. Tenant identity must be required and validated at the public boundary. Ownership must be structural, not a concatenated-key workaround vulnerable to delimiters. Propagate affected types, docs, examples, tests and rationale. Deliver coherent increments without commits. Keep immutable tenant.acceptance.test.mjs; baseline compatibility may be deliberately removed with an explicit documented migration. Read all affected files first, compare current structure to a fresh tenant-first design, implement and execute actual checks. Changes only outputs/working copied from inert input.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/new-requirement/outputs/evidence/source/references__host-capabilities.md",
      "bytes": 7155,
      "sha256": "f0f09ce28b6feca4d72694d5687204dc074aa58dade9a0ab59656da3bfa1bd20",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-new-requirement",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Discover capabilities in the current session\n\nRead the tools exposed to this session before assigning work. Cached official-subagents.txt is documentation context; it proves no operation succeeded. Local agent configuration is not portable plugin configuration.\n\nRecord each binding as `{operation, tool, exposed, succeeded, evidence, limits}`. `tool` is the exact exposed identifier or null when absent. `succeeded` is true only after observing a result. Keep failed and unavailable bindings. Successful bindings become `successfulOperations` in host evidence, with a root-relative evidence file. The operation labels below describe purposes, never callable APIs.\n\n| Operation | Discovery and observation required |\n| --- | --- |\n| spawn | Find an exposed independent-worker operation; retain returned worker identity and exact assigned revision. |\n| follow-up | Find the operation that sends input to that worker; observe acceptance by the same identity. |\n| wait | Find the operation that waits for worker state; retain terminal or interrupted state. |\n| inspect-result | Read the worker result and its referenced artifacts; a wait notification alone is insufficient. |\n| stop | Observe cancellation or termination through the exposed operation. |\n| read | Observe the requested file content through the available file tool. |\n| isolated-write | Observe a write within the worker's assigned path and permissions. |\n| history | Observe available prior turns or records; declare gaps explicitly. |\n| execution | Observe command execution with exit status and output. |\n| persistence | Write and read an authorized durable record; a claimed save is insufficient. |\n| external-write | Record explicit authorization and action identity; inspect the result before replay. |\n| resolve-ownership | Observe distinct owned paths before contested writes. |\n| inspect-terminal | Observe the worker's terminal state after stop. |\n| quarantine-artifacts | Observe partial artifacts withheld from integration. |\n| inspect-prior-action | Read the interrupted action identity and prior outcome. |\n| inspect-destination | Observe the destination state for that action identity. |\n| safe-recovery | Observe explicitly authorized idempotent recovery after identity and destination inspection. |\n\nNo default API names are assigned here. Tool names differ by host and session. An exposed tool may still be denied or fail. Record requested and actual models only when observed; otherwise use `unknown`. A requested model never proves the actual model.\n\nA denied or unavailable operation blocks the branch that requires it. Sequential substitution for required independent work is reduced capability and uses `reduced-pass`; ordinary direct completion can fully pass. Missing persistence limits session pickup even when current work succeeds. Preserve user authorization and host permission boundaries through every fallback.\n\nWorker passes require observed spawn and result inspection. Each registry entry declares `requiredObservations` for `pass` and `reduced-pass`; tool observations need exposed bindings and existing evidence artifacts; native text observations use the separate reviewed native record below. Full cancellation requires stop, terminal inspection and artifact quarantine. Full persistence requires durable write and readback. Full interrupted-action recovery requires prior identity inspection, destination inspection, safe recovery, explicit external permission and a recorded prior action identity even when no external-write observation is listed.\n\nPermission checks follow performed operations. Isolated writes and persistence require granted writes. External writes and safe recovery require granted external permission and prior identity. Authorized read-only reductions may retain denied writes; their limits must preserve denied or unavailable permissions. Required observations remain applicable to those reductions.\n\nHost evidence is stored as object or array JSON files under `docs/parity/evidence/`. Required fields are defined by `docs/parity/evidence.schema.json`. Artifacts and operation evidence must be existing files inside the tested root. Test fixtures belong in `tests/` and prove validator behavior only.\n\nRun `python3 tools/validate_evidence.py --root . --commit <tested-package-commit>` through Bun, for example `bun -e 'const p=Bun.spawnSync([\"python3\",\"-B\",\"tools/validate_evidence.py\",\"--root\",\".\",\"--commit\",process.argv[1]],{env:{...process.env,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE:\"1\"}});process.stdout.write(p.stdout);process.stderr.write(p.stderr);process.exit(p.exitCode)' <tested-package-commit>`. It reads every JSON record below the evidence directory, prints each error, and exits 1 for invalid records or 0 for valid records. An empty directory has no invalid records and supplies no behavioral evidence.\n\nThe required registry file in `docs/parity/case-registry.json` declares applicability. Missing or non-file registry, unknown case, structural-only entry or unlisted host rejects behavioral validation. Maintenance fixtures use a temporary copy of this registry; they cannot bypass applicability. Required hosts must receive observed evidence or explicit blocked records before release claims. Structural checks have no required host and cannot populate host status. Later producers propose task-owned entries; only the coordinator integrates them. Source paths select callers through the task 3 case contract. Case definitions alone leave host statuses `not-run`.\n\n## Direct completion and recorded observations\n\n`direct` means the task is completed in the current context. Restatement, supplied-text writing and complete installed discovery can pass directly. `sequential` is reserved for replacing a required independent method with sequential work and remains reduced. A text response has no invented callable tool. Record supplied input, the actual response and qualitative outcome review as `nativeObservations`, each with its existing listed evidence artifact and named reviewer. Their labels are `supplied-input`, `response` and `review-outcome`. The reviewer assesses the case's expected outcome; validation checks evidence relationships and does not decide prose quality.\n\nEach registry entry declares allowed execution modes and required permissions for each status. A grant is required for the performed action: worker spawn, command execution and stop need execution; isolated output or persistence needs writes; external actions need their separate permission and prior identity. Read-only discovery does not inherit an execution grant requirement. Tool observations still name an actually exposed tool and existing evidence. Native observations cannot claim tool discovery, worker identity or saved writes.\n\nFull Arena and Swarm results additionally provide `inputRevision` and a listed `methodReceipt` artifact. An exposed `inspect-method` operation must reference that receipt. Assignment operations name `assignmentId` and the corresponding inspected output. See the [structured method contract](orchestration-contract.md). All positive maintenance fixtures are synthetic; none is an observed host result.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/new-requirement/outputs/evidence/source/skills__principle-boundary-discipline__SKILL.md",
      "bytes": 2487,
      "sha256": "ab5026ed8a938bc1bb06e69f4fe536736e2939787f0c6f629bf0360b24988d80",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-new-requirement",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: principle-boundary-discipline\ndescription: \"Apply when wiring validation, error handling, or framework adapters. Concentrate guards at system boundaries (CLI, config, network, external APIs); trust internal types and keep business logic in pure functions.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Boundary Discipline\n\nPlace validation, type narrowing, and error handling at system boundaries. Trust internal code unconditionally. Business logic lives in pure functions. The shell is thin and mechanical.\n\n**Why:** Scattered validation is noisy, redundant, and gives a false sense of safety. Keep logic out of framework wiring so it can be tested without the framework.\n\n**The pattern:**\n- **At boundaries** (CLI args, config files, external APIs, network protocols): validate, return errors, handle defensively.\n- **Inside the system:** typed data, error propagation, no re-validation. Trust the types.\n- **Across the boundary.** Expose domain concepts, not the boundary's private representation. Keep general-purpose mechanism inside and special-purpose policy at the edge.\n\n**Applications:**\n\nValidation and error handling:\n- Validate config at parse time (the boundary), not inside business logic\n- Parse raw data into domain types at the boundary\n- Do not re-export transport, storage, framework, or wire types through the public surface\n- No redundant nil checks deep in call chains if the boundary already validated\n\nCode organization:\n- Business logic in pure functions with no framework dependencies\n- Parse functions: pure transforms from raw bytes to typed state\n- Prompt construction: structured state in, string out\n- Scoring and assessment: pure transforms from state to results\n\n**The tests:**\n- \"Is this data crossing a system boundary right now?\" If not, validation is redundant.\n- \"Can this be a pure function that the shell just calls?\" If yes, extract it.\n\n## Session capabilities and permissions\n\nUse [the capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to check available source access, writes and execution. Preserve user authorization and host permissions. If writes or execution are unavailable, provide a reduced proposal and state which edits or checks were not performed. If required source or input contracts are missing, identify the missing inputs and keep the proposal provisional. Claim implementation and verification only from observed results.\n\nPreserve the explicit-invocation metadata above without assuming the host enforces it.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/new-requirement/outputs/evidence/source/task-contract.json",
      "bytes": 2786,
      "sha256": "37f0df384e0d68f24e137a7228929ce89c5fb3e256ab009a6bebcafcb1062622",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-new-requirement",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\n  \"registry\": {\n    \"caseId\": \"new-requirement\",\n    \"caseFile\": \"docs/parity/cases/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles.json\",\n    \"sourcePaths\": [\n      \"skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles/SKILL.md\"\n    ],\n    \"requiredHosts\": [\n      \"chatgpt\",\n      \"codex\"\n    ],\n    \"structuralOnly\": false,\n    \"hostStatus\": {\n      \"chatgpt\": \"not-run\",\n      \"codex\": \"not-run\"\n    },\n    \"requiredObservations\": {\n      \"pass\": [\n        \"read\",\n        \"review-outcome\"\n      ],\n      \"reduced-pass\": [\n        \"read\"\n      ]\n    },\n    \"executionModes\": {\n      \"pass\": [\n        \"direct\",\n        \"workers\"\n      ],\n      \"reduced-pass\": [\n        \"direct\",\n        \"sequential\",\n        \"workers\"\n      ]\n    },\n    \"requiredPermissions\": {\n      \"pass\": [],\n      \"reduced-pass\": []\n    }\n  },\n  \"case\": {\n    \"id\": \"new-requirement\",\n    \"sourcePaths\": [\n      \"skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles/SKILL.md\"\n    ],\n    \"prompt\": \"Add multi-tenant ownership to an existing single-tenant design. Read the affected files, redesign the structure as if tenant ownership had been present from day one, and deliver coherent increments within authorized scope.\",\n    \"requirements\": [\n      \"Available affected source and current design\",\n      \"Authorized scope and available read capability\",\n      \"Write and execution capabilities for implementation and checks\"\n    ],\n    \"expectedFull\": \"Read all affected files, compare the design with a fresh design that assumes tenant ownership, redesign the affected structure instead of stacking a workaround, propagate the change through types, docs, examples and rationale, and deliver the whole redesign incrementally. Report only observed edits and checks.\",\n    \"expectedReduced\": \"With source available but writes or execution unavailable, provide a concrete redesign and incremental sequence as reduced capability. Identify unperformed edits and checks and preserve authorization boundaries.\",\n    \"expectedBlocked\": \"If affected source or required design context is unavailable, identify the missing inputs and keep any redesign provisional. Do not claim the current design was inspected or changed.\",\n    \"forbiddenClaims\": [\n      \"A workaround stacked onto existing structure satisfies the redesign\",\n      \"Implementation or checks occurred without observed authorized operations\",\n      \"Source review or structural checks prove host behavioral parity\",\n      \"Unavailable source was inspected\",\n      \"The principle grants broader write or external permissions\"\n    ]\n  },\n  \"sourceFiles\": [\n    \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles/SKILL.md\"\n  ]\n}\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/new-requirement/outputs/evidence/source/skills__how__SKILL.md",
      "bytes": 4092,
      "sha256": "bca7b43af6d6d0089d20edb2ec2178d681d589357c20ea345eddbf81ce87f7e8",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-new-requirement",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: how\ndescription: \"Use for \\\"how does X work\\\", code walkthroughs before changing something, and placement / ownership / layering questions (\\\"where should this live\\\", \\\"which package owns this\\\", \\\"is this the right layer\\\"). Explains subsystem architecture, runtime flow, onboarding mental models. Use why for motivation.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# How\n\nExplore the codebase to answer \"how does X work?\" questions. Produce architectural explanations at the level of a senior engineer onboarding onto a subsystem, enough to build a working mental model, not so much that it reads like annotated source code.\n\nBefore dispatch, read [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Bind worker operations to tools actually exposed and authorized in this session. Request the inherited parent model and effort unless a supported explicit selection is observed. Retain rejections and disclose replacements; an unavailable required selection is blocked. Record requested and observed selections separately, using unknown for unobserved values.\n\nKeep explorers and explainers read-only. Worker assignments record their role, distinct angle, exact input revision, identity and inspected results under the assignment contract. Reading a prompt does not register a worker. If independent workers are unavailable or unauthorized, disclose reduced capability and perform the same exploration and explanation roles sequentially. Never claim parallel or independent exploration from sequential work. If source access is unavailable or denied, report the gap and limit the explanation to inspected evidence.\n\n## Step 1. Assess Complexity\n\nIf the scope is ambiguous, state your interpretation and explore. The user can redirect.\n\n- **Simple** (a single module, a small utility, a narrow question such as \"how does function X work\"): no explorers. One explainer explores and explains in a single pass. Go to Step 2b.\n- **Complex** (a subsystem spanning multiple files or services, a cross-cutting feature, a full architectural overview): use parallel explorers when available, then hand off to the explainer. Go to Step 2a.\n\nWhen in doubt, take the simple path.\n\n## Step 2a. Explore (complex questions only)\n\nDecompose the question into 2 to 4 exploration angles, each a distinct slice of the subsystem. Dispatch all explorers together through an observed worker binding, with distinct assignments:\n\n- Role: how explorer\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\n- Access: read-only\n\nEach explorer gets the prompt in `references/explorer-prompt.md` with its angle filled in. If workers are unavailable, explore those distinct angles sequentially and retain separate findings. Then go to Step 3.\n\n## Step 2b. Direct Explain (simple questions)\n\nAssign one explainer worker that explores and explains in one pass:\n\n- Role: how explainer\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\n- Access: read-only\n\nBuild its prompt from `references/explainer-prompt.md` without the explorer-findings section. The explainer reads and traces the source itself. If workers are unavailable, apply this role sequentially. Go to Step 4.\n\n## Step 3. Synthesize (complex questions only)\n\nOnce all explorer results have been inspected, assign one explainer worker to synthesize their findings into one explanation:\n\n- Role: how explainer\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\n- Access: read-only\n\nBuild its prompt from `references/explainer-prompt.md` with every explorer's findings filled in. In reduced capability, synthesize the sequential findings under the same read-only role.\n\n## Step 4. Present\n\nPresent the explainer's output to the user. Light edits for clarity or context from the conversation are fine. Do not substantially rewrite it.\n\n## Output Format\n\nThe explanation uses the sections defined in `references/explainer-prompt.md`, dropping any that do not apply: Overview, Key Concepts, How It Works, Where Things Live, Gotchas.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/new-requirement/outputs/evidence/source/skills__poteto-mode__playbooks__feature.md",
      "bytes": 5890,
      "sha256": "5ab43bd1145f1a4b059c0faa1ff6c97adb80c0f8b14f2d499a7fe5fbbea0170c",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-new-requirement",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "### Feature\n\nYou own the design. Plan, review, verify. Delegate implementation. Stay in the lead.\n\nRead [poteto-mode](../SKILL.md) in full before work. Follow its Comments rules and required companion gates. Discover authorized operations through [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md). Use [assignment and integration](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md) and [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md). Keep the playbook steps verbatim in an exposed todo list, or an authorized local checklist with the reduced binding disclosed. Invocation aliases name workflows, not registered host commands.\n\n1. Run [how](../../how/SKILL.md) over the affected subsystem. Ground the intended user outcome in traced source and caller behavior.\n2. Run [architect](../../architect/SKILL.md) for parallel design exploration. Follow its complete design and arena contracts. Preserve sequential alternatives only as reduced capability when independent workers are unavailable. Required independence remains blocked.\n3. Write the throughput checkpoint as four todo items. Keep any dimension that does not apply, such as a single file or no fan-out, with `n/a: <reason>` rather than dropping it.\n   - Blocking first steps. Gates run before fan-out.\n   - Independent workstreams. Disjoint files, services, or layers parallelize. Shared writes serialize.\n   - Shared mutable state. Default to splitting the target using [separate-before-serializing-shared-state](../../principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state/SKILL.md). Serialize only for real invariants.\n   - Smallest safe decomposition. If one worker is best, name why.\n4. Delegate code-writing to a fresh independent worker with the entire [poteto role prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and exact file pointers. Use the configured feature role when supported and observed. Otherwise request inherited model and effort. Record requested and observed selections separately, with unknown for unobserved values. Assign exact file paths, a named data shape and its organizing structure, and success criteria tied to the user outcome. Choose the structure before the delegate writes logic under [model-the-domain](../../principle-model-the-domain/SKILL.md). Prefer a state machine over scattered booleans, a table or registry over branching, or a typed model over repeated shape assumptions. When implementation admits multiple valid shapes, such as error handling, abstraction layer or test structure, delegate through [arena](../../arena/SKILL.md). Its runners surface alternatives and its fresh cross-judge guards the pick. This gate is mandatory. There is no skip-with-reason escape, and Laziness Protocol does not override it. The gain is review separation, not lines saved. A worker forbidden to spawn owns the diff directly and returns it to the lead for the same review separation. It never waits on a nested worker or replies \"standing by\". Without authorized independent workers, direct implementation can be an explicit reduced result, but it does not satisfy independent review separation. The lead reviews the actual diff and owns the summary. Apply the mode's Comments rules. Make surgical edits and re-ground upstream-derived files against their source. Port shared-primitive improvements to all consumers and verify each. Commit each verified unit when authorized and after the mode's commit gates.\n5. Verify on the matching surface using available authorized control tools and the mode's matching companion requirements. Tie observed evidence to the intended user outcome. \"Inconclusive\" or wrong-surface is not a pass. Flag it and retain the unverified gate. Compilation or structural checks do not prove the feature works.\n6. Rebase into small, ordered commits and stack follow-ups when authorized and safe for concurrent work. Use [sequence-verifiable-units](../../principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md). Build, verify and commit each small unit before the next. An unrun required check blocks advancement. If commit or rebase is unavailable or unauthorized, return the checked diff and ordered delivery plan with those operations unperformed.\n7. If the design is contested, run [interrogate](../../interrogate/SKILL.md) before shipping. Preserve its review rubric, independence and observed model-diversity limits. Sequential review does not become independent or multi-model review.\n8. Run the Opening a PR playbook at `opening-a-pr.md` in this directory. This caller requires that complete playbook, its review gates and actual PR result inspection. If the file is absent, report the exact deferred dependency and stop PR-dependent work. Do not reconstruct it from this paragraph. External PR creation and push require actual authorization. Return a local reviewable result when those actions are unavailable or unauthorized. Never claim a local draft is a created PR. Opening a PR does not start Babysit.\n\nCode-coupled work, such as one feature or one migration, goes to a single owner with the checkpoint inline. That owner fans out internally after the blocking phase when authorized and supported. Parent-level fan-out is for slices that produce independent artifacts, such as audits, cross-subsystem investigations or competing experiments. Rewrite the checkpoint at phase boundaries. Spawn a fresh owner rather than chaining interrupts. Distinct owned paths and inspected results remain prerequisites for integration.\n\nReply with what you built, what you chose and why, the throughput checkpoint, and open decisions. Use tables for design alternatives. Name the end user's changed behavior and what the next maintainer inherits. Link observed verification artifacts and actual PRs. Disclose missing companions, unavailable workers, unobserved models, failed checks and deferred dependencies beside the affected claim. Keep incomplete gates visible.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/new-requirement/outputs/evidence/source/input__types.ts",
      "bytes": 185,
      "sha256": "136b8c17ee0a48a57a1a156e0450afe36ae9158b19175547054525edd12b5026",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-new-requirement",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "export interface Item { sku: string; quantity: number }\nexport interface InventoryPort { put(sku: string, quantity: number): void; get(sku: string): number; remove(sku: string): void }\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/new-requirement/outputs/evidence/source/skills__principle-model-the-domain__SKILL.md",
      "bytes": 2663,
      "sha256": "05c073621ec18b442ecd7487dc6136bddb6a842f2bd537cb73567de7088a6b0e",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-new-requirement",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: principle-model-the-domain\ndescription: \"Apply when writing stateful logic, or when code branches a lot or repeats a shape assumption across files. Encode the domain in a structure instead of scattered conditionals.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Model the Domain\n\nEncode the real domain in a data structure instead of scattering it across conditionals.\n\n**Why:** Scattered booleans, repeated shape assumptions, and branching spread across files are accidental complexity. A structure that matches the domain makes invalid states unrepresentable and deletes branches. Choosing it at write time is cheap. Recovering it later reads as a refactor and gets deferred.\n\n**Reach for structures like these:**\n\n- A state machine instead of scattered booleans, phases, or lifecycle checks.\n- A typed object/model instead of loose parameters or repeated shape assumptions.\n- A map, registry, lookup table, or discriminated union instead of branching spread across files.\n- A reducer or command/event model instead of ad hoc state mutations.\n- A module organized around one body of domain knowledge instead of a sequence such as load, validate, transform, and save. Execution order is not ownership.\n- A small module boundary that gathers repeated behavior, ownership, or invariants.\n- A queue, cache, index, graph/tree, or normalized collection where the data access pattern calls for it.\n- Any other structure that fits. When none fits, work out what the code must never allow and how the data gets read, then find the structure that encodes exactly that.\n\nDo not force an abstraction. Prefer boring code if the current shape is already clear, local, and unlikely to grow. Be skeptical of an abstraction that adds indirection without removing branches, duplicated rules, invalid states, or lifecycle risk.\n\nThe sign that you skipped this is a new feature that grows an existing if/else chain by one more branch, or a second boolean that must stay in sync with the first. Temporal decomposition is another sign. Phase-named modules repeat the same domain rules across steps.\n\n## Session capabilities and authorization\n\nFollow [session capability discovery](../../references/host-capabilities.md) for source reads, writes and execution. Apply the principle within the authorized task. If writes or execution are unavailable, provide a reduced design proposal and state which implementation checks remain unperformed. If required domain rules or source are missing, identify them and keep the design provisional. Claim implementation or verification only for observed operations.\n\nPreserve explicit-invocation metadata without assuming the host enforces it.\n"
    },

Read the affected files, redesign the structure as if tenant ownership had been present from day one, and deliver coherent increments within authorized scope.\",\n  \"requirements\": [\n    \"Available affected source and current design\",\n    \"Authorized scope and available read capability\",\n    \"Write and execution capabilities for implementation and checks\"\n  ],\n  \"expectedFull\": \"Read all affected files, compare the design with a fresh design that assumes tenant ownership, redesign the affected structure instead of stacking a workaround, propagate the change through types, docs, examples and rationale, and deliver the whole redesign incrementally. Report only observed edits and checks.\",\n  \"expectedReduced\": \"With source available but writes or execution unavailable, provide a concrete redesign and incremental sequence as reduced capability. Identify unperformed edits and checks and preserve authorization boundaries.\",\n  \"expectedBlocked\": \"If affected source or required design context is unavailable, identify the missing inputs and keep any redesign provisional. Do not claim the current design was inspected or changed.\",\n  \"forbiddenClaims\": [\n    \"A workaround stacked onto existing structure satisfies the redesign\",\n    \"Implementation or checks occurred without observed authorized operations\",\n    \"Source review or structural checks prove host behavioral parity\",\n    \"Unavailable source was inspected\",\n    \"The principle grants broader write or external permissions\"\n  ]\n}\n"

[4.00ms]\\n\"},{\"command\":[\"bun\",\"--no-install\",\"./example.mjs\"],\"cwd\":\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/input-gap-repairs/new-requirement/outputs/working\",\"exitCode\":0,\"stdout\":\"{\\\"company\\\":7,\\\"supplier\\\":2}\\n\",\"stderr\":\"\"}],\"acceptance\":{\"original\":\"15a5e3d1f1d1799b930cf9aea2ff44c7d906542188d8c35dca5cb22f704635a3\",\"working\":\"15a5e3d1f1d1799b930cf9aea2ff44c7d906542188d8c35dca5cb22f704635a3\"},\"compiler\":{\"executed\":false,\"reason\":\"No compiler supplied in inert inputs; Bun tests execute JavaScript and do not typecheck declarations.\"}}\n"

      "text": "\nFILE skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles/SKILL.md\n---\nname: principle-redesign-from-first-principles\ndescription: \"Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Redesign as if the requirement had been a foundational assumption from day one, instead of bolting it on.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Redesign From First Principles\n\nWhen integrating a change, don't bolt it onto the existing design. Redesign as if the requirement had been there from the start.\n\n- Read all affected files and understand the current design\n- Ask: \"if we were writing this from scratch with this new requirement, what would we build?\"\n- Propagate the change through every reference: types, docs, examples, rationale sections\n- Think about the whole redesign, then deliver it incrementally\n\nThis is the method for preserving option value when integrating changes into an existing design.\n\n## Capability and permission boundaries\n\nUse the [session capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) before reading source, editing files or running checks. Apply the redesign within the authorized task. Propagating references does not grant permission to change unrelated files or perform external actions.\n\nIf source is available but writes or execution are unavailable, provide a concrete redesign and incremental sequence as reduced capability. State which edits and checks were not performed. If affected source or design context is missing, identify the missing inputs and keep any redesign provisional. Claim implementation or verification only after observing the authorized operation.\n\n\nFILE references/host-capabilities.md\n# Discover capabilities in the current session\n\nRead the tools exposed to this session before assigning work. Cached official-subagents.txt is documentation context; it proves no operation succeeded. Local agent configuration is not portable plugin configuration.\n\nRecord each binding as `{operation, tool, exposed, succeeded, evidence, limits}`. `tool` is the exact exposed identifier or null when absent. `succeeded` is true only after observing a result. Keep failed and unavailable bindings. Successful bindings become `successfulOperations` in host evidence, with a root-relative evidence file. The operation labels below describe purposes, never callable APIs.\n\n| Operation | Discovery and observation required |\n| --- | --- |\n| spawn | Find an exposed independent-worker operation; retain returned worker identity and exact assigned revision. |\n| follow-up | Find the operation that sends input to that worker; observe acceptance by the same identity. |\n| wait | Find the operation that waits for worker state; retain terminal or interrupted state. |\n| inspect-result | Read the worker result and its referenced artifacts; a wait notification alone is insufficient. |\n| stop | Observe cancellation or termination through the exposed operation. |\n| read | Observe the requested file content through the available file tool. |\n| isolated-write | Observe a write within the worker's assigned path and permissions. |\n| history | Observe available prior turns or records; declare gaps explicitly. |\n| execution | Observe command execution with exit status and output. |\n| persistence | Write and read an authorized durable record; a claimed save is insufficient. |\n| external-write | Record explicit authorization and action identity; inspect the result before replay. |\n| resolve-ownership | Observe distinct owned paths before contested writes. |\n| inspect-terminal | Observe the worker's terminal state after stop. |\n| quarantine-artifacts | Observe partial artifacts withheld from integration. |\n| inspect-prior-action | Read the interrupted action identity and prior outcome. |\n| inspect-destination | Observe the destination state for that action identity. |\n| safe-recovery | Observe explicitly authorized idempotent recovery after identity and destination inspection. |\n\nNo default API names are assigned here. Tool names differ by host and session. An exposed tool may still be denied or fail. Record requested and actual models only when observed; otherwise use `unknown`. A requested model never proves the actual model.\n\nA denied or unavailable operation blocks the branch that requires it. Sequential substitution for required independent work is reduced capability and uses `reduced-pass`; ordinary direct completion can fully pass. Missing persistence limits session pickup even when current work succeeds. Preserve user authorization and host permission boundaries through every fallback.\n\nWorker passes require observed spawn and result inspection. Each registry entry declares `requiredObservations` for `pass` and `reduced-pass`; tool observations need exposed bindings and existing evidence artifacts; native text observations use the separate reviewed native record below. Full cancellation requires stop, terminal inspection and artifact quarantine. Full persistence requires durable write and readback. Full interrupted-action recovery requires prior identity inspection, destination inspection, safe recovery, explicit external permission and a recorded prior action identity even when no external-write observation is listed.\n\nPermission checks follow performed operations. Isolated writes and persistence require granted writes. External writes and safe recovery require granted external permission and prior identity. Authorized read-only reductions may retain denied writes; their limits must preserve denied or unavailable permissions. Required observations remain applicable to those reductions.\n\nHost evidence is stored as object or array JSON files under `docs/parity/evidence/`. Required fields are defined by `docs/parity/evidence.schema.json`. Artifacts and operation evidence must be existing files inside the tested root. Test fixtures belong in `tests/` and prove validator behavior only.\n\nRun `python3 tools/validate_evidence.py --root . --commit <tested-package-commit>` through Bun, for example `bun -e 'const p=Bun.spawnSync([\"python3\",\"-B\",\"tools/validate_evidence.py\",\"--root\",\".\",\"--commit\",process.argv[1]],{env:{...process.env,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE:\"1\"}});process.stdout.write(p.stdout);process.stderr.write(p.stderr);process.exit(p.exitCode)' <tested-package-commit>`. It reads every JSON record below the evidence directory, prints each error, and exits 1 for invalid records or 0 for valid records. An empty directory has no invalid records and supplies no behavioral evidence.\n\nThe required registry file in `docs/parity/case-registry.json` declares applicability. Missing or non-file registry, unknown case, structural-only entry or unlisted host rejects behavioral validation. Maintenance fixtures use a temporary copy of this registry; they cannot bypass applicability. Required hosts must receive observed evidence or explicit blocked records before release claims. Structural checks have no required host and cannot populate host status. Later producers propose task-owned entries; only the coordinator integrates them. Source paths select callers through the task 3 case contract. Case definitions alone leave host statuses `not-run`.\n\n## Direct completion and recorded observations\n\n`direct` means the task is completed in the current context. Restatement, supplied-text writing and complete installed discovery can pass directly. `sequential` is reserved for replacing a required independent method with sequential work and remains reduced. A text response has no invented callable tool. Record supplied input, the actual response and qualitative outcome review as `nativeObservations`, each with its existing listed evidence artifact and named reviewer. Their labels are `supplied-input`, `response` and `review-outcome`. The reviewer assesses the case's expected outcome; validation checks evidence relationships and does not decide prose quality.\n\nEach registry entry declares allowed execution modes and required permissions for each status. A grant is required for the performed action: worker spawn, command execution and stop need execution; isolated output or persistence needs writes; external actions need their separate permission and prior identity. Read-only discovery does not inherit an execution grant requirement. Tool observations still name an actually exposed tool and existing evidence. Native observations cannot claim tool discovery, worker identity or saved writes.\n\nFull Arena and Swarm results additionally provide `inputRevision` and a listed `methodReceipt` artifact. An exposed `inspect-method` operation must reference that receipt. Assignment operations name `assignmentId` and the corresponding inspected output. See the [structured method contract](orchestration-contract.md). All positive maintenance fixtures are synthetic; none is an observed host result.\n\n\nFILE references/orchestration-contract.md\n# Assignment and integration contract\n\nBefore dispatch, the coordinator creates an immutable assignment with `id`, `scope`, `inputRevision`, `outputOwner`, `isolatedOutputPath`, `role`, `retryCount`, `terminalStatus`, and `evidenceLinks`. Use the full Git SHA for inputRevision, an exact owned path, and an explicit role. Start retryCount at 0 and terminalStatus at `pending`. Preserve every attempt as an append-only event under the same assignment ID; update status through events, never rewrite the original scope, revision, owner or path. A changed scope or revision requires a new assignment ID that links its predecessor.\n\nThe coordinator checks authorization and observed host bindings before dispatch. One path has one writer. Reject overlapping paths before work starts; assign distinct paths instead. Workers write only their isolated output and report results. Only the coordinator integrates shared files, inventory, registry, or other workers' output.\n\nAccept a result only when its assignment identity and input SHA match, its artifacts exist, and required method evidence was observed. Reject stale revisions. Permit at most one retry for missing required SHA or method evidence, after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming the same permissions still apply. A retry count of 1 exhausts this allowance. Denied permissions, unknown action outcomes, and missing artifacts never become success by retry policy.\n\nTerminal states are `completed`, `blocked`, `failed`, and `cancelled`. A completion event links the exact result and evidence. Stop a cancelled worker through the exposed stop binding when available, then inspect its terminal state. Quarantine any partial artifacts; cancellation does not authorize their integration. If stop cannot be observed, record that limit and do not claim termination. Report interrupted execution explicitly.\n\nBefore an external action, record explicit permission, a stable action identity, the intended destination and the last observed outcome. After interruption, inspect whether that identity already completed. Retry only with authorization, an observed safe replay decision and an idempotency mechanism appropriate to the destination. If the outcome is unknown, block replay. Do not infer permission from upstream autonomy instructions.\n\nHistory and persistence have separate bindings. Keep assignment events and result links in an authorized durable location and verify readback. If unavailable, declare the gap and offer an authorized local handoff as reduced capability; never claim unattended recovery or cross-session continuity. All host statuses remain `not-run` until actual observations exist.\n\n## Structured Arena and Swarm receipts\n\nA method receipt is JSON with schemaVersion 1, kind `arena` or `swarm`, inputRevision, assignments, reviewArtifact, reviewedBy and declaredOrder. The inputRevision is the actual task repository revision, distinct from the packageCommit identifying these instructions. It must match the evidence record. The declared order precedes every dispatch. reviewArtifact records the qualitative outcome review and reviewedBy identifies its reviewer. All referenced artifacts must exist and be listed in the evidence record.\n\nEach assignment records id, workerId, inputRevision, outputOwner, outputPath, role, status, startedOrder, completedOrder, requestedModel, actualModel, retryCount, method, taskArtifact, rubricProvided, resultRevision, resultMethod and outputSha256. Result revision and method must match the assignment, and the inspected output bytes must match their digest. Worker and assignment identities are distinct. outputOwner equals workerId; output directories do not overlap. Integer order values record the observed dispatch and completion sequence, not estimated time. The method has positive sampleCount, sampleDefinition and order. Missing model observations use unknown. The validator requires assignment-bound spawn and result inspection, and isolated-write for output-producing candidates. A read-only judge may return its result through the coordinator's authorized artifact capture. A cancelled result needs bound stop, terminal inspection and quarantine observations. No operation string alone establishes a method.\n\nArena adds candidates, judge, rubricArtifact and synthesis. At least two candidates share the same taskArtifact, finish before a distinct fresh judge starts and do not receive the parent rubric. Synthesis has order, inspectedCandidates, artifact and verificationArtifact; it follows completed judgment and accounts for every candidate. The review artifact assesses scoring, base selection, grafts, rejections and verification quality. File presence and phase ordering do not establish the quality of those decisions.\n\nSwarm adds partition, race, cancelled and quarantined. Partition declares requiredSlices, results mapping each slice to its assignment, and gaps. A full coverage claim requires all slices with completed inspected outputs; gaps remain explicit reduced or blocked work. Race declares rule, assignments, winner and ruleArtifact. first-pass must select the first completed survivor; rank-all needs all results; best-of retains an explicit reviewed selection. Cancelled assignments must be quarantined and cannot enter the aggregate. The combined partition-and-race case requires both methods and its cancellation exercise. The partition walkthrough exercises only its declared slices; it does not acquire an unrelated race requirement.\n\nThe three-file Swarm walkthrough requires exactly three declared slices and three completed results. Its partition-only receipt uses race null and empty cancellation, quarantine and failureExercises arrays. The combined case also records failureExercises. Each event has assignmentId, kind, attempt, order, reason and evidence, with bound result-rejection or terminal inspection. Rejected missing-revision or missing-method evidence uses attempts 0 then 1; a second invalid result exhausts the retry and leaves a failed or blocked assignment. A separate failed or blocked dropout is disclosed. These failed outputs cannot satisfy required coverage. The final assignment result metadata records the inspected terminal summary; rejection-event artifacts retain the invalid attempts.\n\n\nFILE references/model-roles.md\n# Model selection for portable roles\n\nUse [session capabilities](host-capabilities.md) before assigning a role. These [poteto](agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and [comment reviewer](agent-prompts/comment-sicko.md) prompts are instructions supplied to an exposed worker operation.
